I bowl every Thursday night down at Strike Zone. Still look forward to it every single week. Though, I gotta tell you, last week was a total mess. We're in the middle of the fourth frame, right? We're up against the guys from the plumbing supply warehouse. And the pinsetter on lane 7 just completely jams. The sweeper bar gets stuck halfway down. So we're standing there for forty minutes waiting for the mechanic to come fix it. And the kid they got working the back now—he can't be more than eighteen, got his headphones in, barely even looking at the machine. \n\nI told Gary, he's the manager there, I said, \"Gary, you're paying this kid by the hour to stare at a conveyor belt.\" Back when I was younger, before I got my CDL and started driving, I used to do some maintenance work at a roller rink, and you actually had to hustle. You couldn't just stand around. Anyway, the whole delay completely threw my rhythm off. I rolled a 135. Usually I'm hitting at least a 180, 190 on a good night. My buddy Mike wouldn't let me hear the end of it. \n\nBut, uh... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, losing pleasure in things. No, I still like doing my stuff. The fun things are still fun. It's really just that day-to-day heavy feeling I was talking about.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, would you say that during the past few years, there were times when the things you used to enjoy didn’t feel pleasurable at all, even for a few days in a row?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? Or something real close to it? Like I said, no, I don't get like that. If I like doing something, I still like it. It's not like I just wake up one day and hate grilling or bowling. \n\nSpeaking of grilling, though, I was at the hardware store this weekend looking at those new pellet smokers. Have you seen those things? You can control the temperature from an app on your phone now. It's ridiculous. I was talking to the guy in the apron, asking him how the auger feeds the pellets, because I was thinking, if that jams up while you're at work, your whole brisket is ruined. And brisket is not cheap right now. Have you seen meat prices lately? I went to the butcher down on 4th Street—Sal's, been going there for twenty years—and a decent packer brisket was almost ninety bucks. I told Sal, I said, \"Sal, am I buying the cow or just renting it?\" He didn't think it was funny. He's been real grumpy since his son decided not to take over the shop and went to culinary school instead. Wants to make fancy foams and little smears of sauce on big white plates. \n\nBut anyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right, enjoying things. Yeah, no, I still enjoy my hobbies and stuff. It's just that heavy feeling I told you about, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, would you say that there were times when the things you used to enjoy didn’t feel pleasurable at all, even if you still did them?\n\nPatient: Doc, are you pulling my leg? I swear we just went over this twice. I don't lose interest in things. I still like what I like. \n\nIt's like my truck. I've been driving the same rig for, what, six years now? A Freightliner Cascadia. Beautiful machine when she's running right. I still love getting behind the wheel, hitting the open road, you know? Though, I'll tell you, the dispatchers lately have been driving me up the wall. They put me on this new route last month, taking me all the way up through the mountains. And the weather up there? Unpredictable. Last Tuesday, I'm hauling a load of automotive parts—mostly brake pads and rotors, heavy stuff—and out of nowhere, it starts sleeting. Just sheets of ice coming down. I had to pull over at this little diner off Route 9. \n\nPlace looked like it hadn't been updated since 1985. Neon sign buzzing, vinyl booths all cracked. But let me tell you, they made the best open-faced turkey sandwich I've had in a decade. Real roasted turkey, none of that deli-sliced garbage, and gravy that actually tasted like meat. The waitress, Shirley, she told me they roast a whole bird every single morning. I said to her, \"Shirley, if I wasn't married, I'd ask you to run away with me just for this gravy.\" She just laughed, said she hears that from every trucker who comes through. \n\nBut, uh, what was I saying? Oh, right. The truck. Yeah, I still enjoy driving. I still enjoy my food. I still enjoy my bowling and my grilling. It's just that heavy, tired feeling I get, like I'm dragging a chain around all day. That's it. None of this losing pleasure stuff you keep asking about.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you ever had periods where you truly didn’t feel any enjoyment, even from things you usually like doing, not just a heavy feeling but a complete lack of pleasure?\n\nPatient: Doc, seriously? Are you reading off a script or something? Because I feel like I'm taking crazy pills here. We've talked about this, what, four times now? No. I don't lose my enjoyment of things. If I like it, I like it. \n\nI mean, take my dog, Buster. He's a golden retriever mix, absolute goofball. I always enjoy taking him for his walk in the evenings. Even when it's raining, though I gotta say, the smell of wet dog in my truck cab is not my favorite thing. I had to buy this special upholstery cleaner just to get the smell out of the passenger seat because my wife, Linda, she let him ride up front on the way back from the vet last month. He had to get some shots, you know, the annual ones. And the vet, Dr. Henderson, he's a good guy, but he talks your ear off about his golf game. I don't even play golf. I told him, I said, \"Doc, if I wanted to walk around a big lawn hitting a little ball, I'd just buy a riding mower and a croquet set.\" \n\nLinda thought that was hilarious, but then she let Buster put his muddy paws all over the dashboard. I spent three hours on Sunday detailing the inside of that cab. Used the good armor-all wipes and everything. You really gotta take care of your vehicles, you know? It's an investment. \n\nBut... yeah. No. I don't get that complete lack of pleasure thing. I still enjoy my dog, my truck, my bowling. It's just that tired, dragging feeling I keep trying to tell you about. Can we talk about that instead?\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you ever felt like the things you do, even the ones you say you still enjoy, just feel empty or meaningless, like they don’t really matter to you anymore?\n\nPatient: Doc, I feel like we're just going in circles here. Empty? Meaningless? No, not really. I mean, bowling matters to me because it gets me out of the house and I get to see the guys. It's not like... I don't know, trying to find the meaning of life at the bowling alley. \n\nSpeaking of the alley, though, did I tell you they changed the oil pattern on the lanes last week? Totally threw my hook off. I usually throw a 15-pound Brunswick, right? Got it drilled specifically for my grip down at the pro shop by this guy, Artie. Artie’s been drilling balls since the seventies. Guy’s missing half his left index finger—says it was a band saw accident, but rumor is he owed money to the wrong people back in the day. Anyway, they laid down this new oil pattern, they call it the \"Chameleon\" or something stupid like that, and my ball just wouldn't bite the lane. I was leaving ten-pins all night. Drives me absolutely nuts when you hit the pocket perfect and that one pin just stands there mocking you. \n\nMy buddy Dave—he drives a local route for a beverage distributor—he was having the same problem. We ended up just drinking pitchers of cheap draft beer and complaining about the management. The new manager, this kid who looks like he's about twelve, he comes over and tries to tell us we're using the wrong approach. I almost lost it. I told him I've been bowling since before he was born. \n\nBut, uh, anyway. No, things don't feel empty or meaningless. I just get tired, like I said. Just that heavy feeling. Are we gonna talk about that, or are you gonna ask me about not liking things again?\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you ever felt like the things you do, even the ones you say you still enjoy, just feel empty or meaningless, like they don’t really matter to you anymore?\n\nPatient: Doc, I swear, you're asking me the exact same thing again. I don't get why we keep circling back to this. \n\nI mean, if things felt meaningless, why would I even bother getting up in the morning? Why would I bother getting the truck washed? Last time, they left soap residue all over the chrome exhaust stacks. I had to spend an hour buffing it out myself. Chrome's not cheap, you know? And if you let that soap sit, it ruins the finish. \n\nThe new place, though, they actually use a good wax. The guy running it, Hector, he said they import the wax from somewhere in Europe. I don't know if I believe him, but it does leave a nice shine. He’s got this setup where they do the undercarriage wash for free if you buy the premium package. I always get the premium because, with all the salt they put on the roads up north, if you don't keep that undercarriage clean, rust will eat right through the frame. I saw a guy last winter, his whole back axle just about rusted off because he never washed the salt off. \n\nBut look, no, I don't feel empty or meaningless. I just feel tired. That's it. Doc, I... I literally just answered this. Are you even listening to me? I don't lose interest in things. I don't stop enjoying things. Not for a few days, not for a few hours. 

I mean, if I lost interest in things, I wouldn't care about my garden, right? And I care a lot about my garden. I spent all of Saturday putting in these new tomato plants. Beefsteaks, mostly, but I'm trying out this heirloom variety this year, Cherokee Purple. Supposed to have a really rich flavor. You gotta be careful with them, though, because they're prone to blossom end rot if you don't keep the watering consistent. My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she overwatered hers last year and lost the whole crop. I told her, I said, "Edna, you're drowning the poor things." She's a nice lady, but she doesn't know the first thing about soil drainage. She uses that cheap topsoil from the big box stores. You gotta get the good compost, work it into the dirt before you even think about planting. 

I get my compost from this guy down on Route 9, sells it out of the back of his pickup. Smells awful, but man, does it make the tomatoes grow. 

So, no. I don't lose interest in things. I enjoy my tomatoes, I enjoy my dog, I enjoy my bowling. I'm just tired all the time. That's what I'm here for. Can we please talk about why I feel like I'm dragging a hundred-pound weight around all day?

I get through the routes, and I still show up for my volunteer shifts, too. But even with all that, it’s like... I’m just going through the motions instead of really being in my own life. Just sort of coasting on autopilot, kind of like when I'm stuck in that bumper-to-bumper traffic just staring at the brake lights in front of me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with that heavy, low feeling most days?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... it's been about three years now. Ever since my father passed away. We used to go fishing out at Lake George every summer, you know? He had this old aluminum boat, nothing fancy, just a little outboard motor that took about ten pulls to get started on a good day. But man, we'd sit out there for hours. Sometimes we wouldn't even catch anything, just swatting mosquitoes and eating those dry ham sandwiches my mom used to pack. She always put way too much mustard on them, but you didn't complain, you just ate it. He loved that boat. We had to sell it after he died, which was a real shame, but my sister didn't have room for it and my apartment complex doesn't allow trailers in the parking lot. They're real strict about the parking rules there, actually. Just last week they towed my neighbor's sedan because his tags were two days expired. Two days! Can you believe that? \n\nBut yeah... it's been about three years since he died, and that feeling just kind of settled in and never really left.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t enjoy things as much as you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you once liked?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I still like doing the things I usually do. Like, I still enjoy grilling out on the weekends when I'm not on the road. I swear they make them too bright these days. I tried putting a piece of tape over it, but then I couldn't read the time at all. It was supposed to have a radio built in, but the antenna is just this flimsy little wire that hangs off the back, and the only station I can pick up clearly is some talk radio show out of the next county over. The host on there just yells about politics for three hours straight. Who wants to listen to that at four in the morning? \n\nSo anyway, I end up just tossing and turning. As for the other thing you asked... no, I don't feel like I'm not worth much or anything like that. I know I do a good job at work and I help out where I can. It's just that heavy feeling, and being so damn tired all the time from not sleeping right.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or that your thoughts feel slowed down, like you're in a fog?\n\nPatient: Well, moving slow... physically I'm about the same, I reckon. Like, I can still do my job, but lately I catch myself rereading the delivery times on my manifest, or I'll just stand there staring at the schedule board at the depot longer than I really should. I don't know who picked it out, probably someone from corporate who's never even been to our branch. It's supposed to be \"energizing\" or something, but it just looks like cheap mustard. And they replaced the old coffee machine, too. The new one uses those little plastic pods. You get, what, maybe a thimble full of coffee out of one of those things? And it tastes like burnt plastic. Regional risk factors! I live in a town of ten thousand people, the biggest risk is hitting a deer on County Road 9. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I still enjoy things. Can you tell me what’s been going on that brought you here today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess I came in because... for a long time now, I’ve just had this heavy, low feeling sitting on me most days. I mean, I still get through my delivery routes just fine. I drive a truck, mostly out on the interstate. Traffic has been an absolute nightmare lately, especially with all that construction they're doing over on the east side. It feels like they've been working on that same stretch of highway for five years, and it just backs up for miles every single afternoon. You'd think they'd figure out a better way to pave a road by now without shutting down two lanes, right? It really messes up my schedule. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... I get through the routes, and I still show up for my volunteer shifts, too. But even with all that, it’s like... I’m just going through the motions instead of really being in my own life. Just sort of coasting on autopilot, kind of like when I'm stuck in that bumper-to-bumper traffic just staring at the brake lights in front of me.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, with that heavy, low feeling most days?\n\nPatient: Well, let's see... it's been about three years now. Ever since my father passed away. We used to go fishing out at Lake George every summer, you know? He had this old aluminum boat, nothing fancy, just a little outboard motor that took about ten pulls to get started on a good day. But man, we'd sit out there for hours. Sometimes we wouldn't even catch anything, just swatting mosquitoes and eating those dry ham sandwiches my mom used to pack. She always put way too much mustard on them, but you didn't complain, you just ate it. He loved that boat. We had to sell it after he died, which was a real shame, but my sister didn't have room for it and my apartment complex doesn't allow trailers in the parking lot. They're real strict about the parking rules there, actually. Just last week they towed my neighbor's sedan because his tags were two days expired. Two days! Can you believe that? \n\nBut yeah... it's been about three years since he died, and that feeling just kind of settled in and never really left.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you don’t enjoy things as much as you used to, or that you’ve lost interest in activities you once liked?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I still like doing the things I usually do. Like, I still enjoy grilling out on the weekends when I'm not on the road. I've got this old Weber kettle grill... nothing beats charcoal, you know? Though, man, the price of a bag of briquettes lately is just highway robbery. \n\nI went down to the hardware store the other day—the little local one on 4th Street, not the big chain. Old man Henderson used to run it, but his son took over a couple years back. The son's alright, I guess, but he rearranged the whole damn store. Used to be you'd walk in and the fasteners and screws were right there on your left. Now you gotta walk all the way past the garden hoses and the paint mixer just to find a simple galvanized bolt. It makes absolutely no sense. I spent twenty minutes in there last Tuesday just looking for a replacement wingnut for my lawnmower handle. Just wandering the aisles while this kid at the register was glued to his phone. \n\nBut, uh... what was the question? Oh, right, losing interest. No, I still like my hobbies and my volunteer work and everything. I haven't stopped liking them. I just... I still have that heavy feeling while I'm doing them, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep or appetite, or do you ever feel like you're not worth much or that life isn't worth living?\n\nPatient: Well, my appetite's been about the same, really. I still eat regular. But sleep... sleep's a whole different story. On nights before a run, I’ll just lie there in the dark, thinking about the next day’s stops, going over the route in my head. Or I'll wake up around four in the morning and just stare at the ceiling, not really able to get back to sleep. My alarm clock has those bright red LED numbers, you know? I swear they make them too bright these days. I tried putting a piece of tape over it, but then I couldn't read the time at all. I bought it at that same hardware store I was telling you about, actually. It was supposed to have a radio built in, but the antenna is just this flimsy little wire that hangs off the back, and the only station I can pick up clearly is some talk radio show out of the next county over. The host on there just yells about politics for three hours straight. Who wants to listen to that at four in the morning? \n\nSo anyway, I end up just tossing and turning. By the time I actually make it to work, even if I'm on time, I feel like I’m starting the day already completely worn down. As for the other thing you asked... no, I don't feel like I'm not worth much or anything like that. I know I do a good job at work and I help out where I can. It's just that heavy feeling, and being so damn tired all the time from not sleeping right.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever noticed yourself moving more slowly than usual, or that your thoughts feel slowed down, like you're in a fog?\n\nPatient: Well, moving slow... physically I'm about the same, I reckon. I still load and unload the truck just fine. But my thoughts... yeah, they do feel a bit foggy sometimes. Like, I can still do my job, but lately I catch myself rereading the delivery times on my manifest, or I'll just stand there staring at the schedule board at the depot longer than I really should. It's like my mind just doesn't lock onto things the way it used to. \n\nSpeaking of the depot, they just repainted the whole breakroom this awful shade of yellow. I don't know who picked it out, probably someone from corporate who's never even been to our branch. It's supposed to be \"energizing\" or something, but it just looks like cheap mustard. And they replaced the old coffee machine, too. The new one uses those little plastic pods. You get, what, maybe a thimble full of coffee out of one of those things? And it tastes like burnt plastic. I miss the old drip machine. Sure, the pot was usually half-burnt by 10 AM, but at least it tasted like real coffee, you know? \n\nBut yeah, my mind just... it wanders, or it just won't focus when I'm looking at the paperwork. I have to read the same line two or three times before it actually sinks in.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been depressed most of the day, nearly every day, rather than just feeling that heavy low feeling you described?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose so. It's most days. I mean, that's really why I came in, because for a long time now, I’ve been getting through my routes and my volunteer shifts just fine, but... well, speaking of volunteering, I help out over at the community center on Thursday nights. Usually setting up the tables for the senior bingo games. And you wouldn't believe how particular some of those folks get about their bingo cards. If you hand Mrs. Gable a blue card instead of a red one, she'll chew your ear off for a good twenty minutes. And the daubers—we had a whole box of those ink daubers dry out last week because somebody left them right next to the radiator in the basement storage closet. I told the director, I said, \"Dave, you can't put ink next to a boiler,\" but nobody ever listens. It's just a waste of good money, having to throw out a dozen brand new markers. \n\nBut, uh, what was I saying? Right, the mood thing. Yeah, that heavy, low feeling is sitting on me most days, pretty much all day. Even when I'm doing my route or setting up the hall, it just feels like I’m going through the motions instead of really being in my own life, you know? Like I'm just on autopilot.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the past few years, have you ever felt like you couldn’t enjoy things, even things you used to like doing?\n\nPatient: Well, I wouldn't say I can't enjoy things. I still like doing stuff, mostly. Like, I still enjoy watching the game on Sundays. Though, I tell you, the broadcasts lately are just one long commercial break. You watch for five minutes, and then it's three minutes of ads for trucks and insurance. And those insurance commercials with the little cartoon animals? I don't get it. What does a talking lizard have to do with my car insurance premium? My premium went up forty dollars this year, by the way. I called my agent, Bob—I've known Bob for fifteen years, our kids played Little League together—and I asked him, \"Bob, I haven't had a ticket since 1998, why is my rate going up?\" And he just gave me some runaround about inflation and regional risk factors. Regional risk factors! I live in a town of ten thousand people, the biggest risk is hitting a deer on County Road 9. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I still enjoy things. I don't think I've lost interest in stuff I like doing. I just feel tired while I'm doing them, I guess. But I still like 'em.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt a noticeable decrease in your energy or felt fatigued almost every day, even when you’re not doing anything particularly strenuous?\n\nPatient: Fatigued? No, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I get tired after a long shift, sure, but my energy hasn't really dropped out of nowhere. I still have enough in the tank to get my chores done. Like, I was out washing my personal truck just this past Saturday. I usually go to that self-serve bay over on Route 17, but they just raised the price on the coin machines. It used to be two quarters for three minutes, now it's a dollar fifty just to turn the wand on. And the soap pressure is terrible lately. Barely gets the road grime off the quarter panels. My neighbor, Gary, he bought himself one of those fancy gas-powered pressure washers, and he's out there blasting his driveway every other weekend. Makes a racket like a jet engine right at 8 AM. I asked him if I could borrow it once to do my patio and he gave me this whole twenty-minute speech about how it voids the warranty if someone else operates it. Just ridiculous. \n\nBut, uh... anyway, what was I saying? Right, no, my energy is mostly fine. I don't feel drained or anything unless I've actually been working hard all day.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, would you say that the things you still say you like doing feel less enjoyable or rewarding than they used to, even if you still do them?\n\nPatient: Less enjoyable? No, I don't think so. I mean, if I'm watching the game or tinkering in the garage, I still get the same kick out of it. Speaking of the garage, I finally got around to organizing my socket sets this weekend. You know how it is, you buy a set and within a month the 10-millimeter is just gone. Vanished into thin air. I swear there's a black hole in my workbench that just swallows 10-millimeter sockets. I ended up going down to the hardware store—the local one, not the big box place, because the big box place never has anybody working in the aisles when you need them. You wander around for half an hour looking for a specific bolt and the only person you find is a kid who looks like he's still in high school and he just points vaguely toward aisle 14. Anyway, the local guy, Frank, he knows exactly where everything is. He's been running that store since I moved here. Though he's talking about selling the place, which is a shame. \n\nBut, uh, no, to answer your question, things still feel just as rewarding when I do them. I haven't noticed them being any less enjoyable.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself doing things more slowly, or feeling like it takes extra effort to get started on tasks, even simple ones like getting out of bed or taking a shower?\n\nPatient: Slower? No, I wouldn't say that. I don't have any trouble getting out of bed or taking a shower. I hop right in every morning. Though, I'll tell you, the hot water in my house takes absolutely forever to kick in lately. I think my water heater is on its last legs. It's a Rheem, installed it back in '08. Supposed to last ten years, so I guess I got my money's worth, but I priced out a new one at the supply house and they want like twelve hundred bucks for it now. Plus installation! \n\nI talked to my plumber, Dave—good guy, I went to high school with his older brother—and I said, Dave, I'm just heating water, not launching a space shuttle. Why is it twelve hundred dollars? And he starts going on about energy efficiency ratings and Wi-Fi modules. Who needs Wi-Fi on a water heater? I just want the water to be hot when I turn the knob. I don't need my phone to tell me the tank is full. Just ridiculous how everything has a computer chip in it now. Even the new rigs I drive for work, the dashboard looks like an airplane cockpit, and half the time the sensors just beep at you for no reason. \n\nBut, uh... yeah, no, I'm not moving any slower. I get up and get going just fine. It doesn't take me any extra effort.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like things that used to be fun or pleasurable just feel like chores or don’t feel rewarding anymore, even if you still do them?\n\nPatient: I feel like we just talked about this, Doc. But no, honestly, things don't feel like chores unless they actually *are* chores, you know? The fun stuff is still fun. \n\nLike grilling. I was out on the back deck firing up the Weber just this past Sunday. Still love doing it. Though, I gotta tell you, the price of ground beef right now is an absolute joke. I went over to the ShopRite—usually they have that 80/20 chuck on sale, the big family pack. But now? It's pushing seven bucks a pound. For ground beef! I saw Carl behind the meat counter—I've known the guy for years, used to coach his oldest boy in Little League back in the day—and I said, Carl, what is going on, are these cows eating gold flakes? And he just gives me this tired look and says, \"supply chain.\" \n\nI swear, that's the excuse for everything now. You go to buy a pack of hamburger buns and they're out of stock, supply chain. You try to get a replacement air filter for the rig at work, supply chain. It's just a blank check for these companies to jack up the prices if you ask me. I ended up just buying a pack of hot dogs instead because I wasn't about to pay steak prices for burgers. \n\nBut, uh... what were we saying? Right, no, the fun stuff is still rewarding. I still get a kick out of it. It's just paying for it that feels like a chore.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been depressed most of the day, nearly every day, rather than just feeling that heavy low feeling you described?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, I guess that's really why I'm here. It's not like I'm crying or anything, but for a long time now, there's just this... this heavy, low feeling sitting on me most days. I still get through my routes at work, I still do my volunteer shifts at the firehouse, but it just feels like I'm going through the motions, you know? Like I'm not really *in* my own life. \n\nSpeaking of the firehouse, we had the annual pancake breakfast fundraiser this past weekend. Usually, we get a huge turnout, line out the door. But this year, the town decided to schedule the spring festival on the exact same morning. Can you believe that? The mayor's office didn't even bother to check the community calendar. So we've got all these guys flipping flapjacks, mixing up gallons of batter, and half the town is over at the park looking at craft booths. I told the chief, I said, \"Chief, we need to get somebody on the town council who actually talks to the local organizations.\" Because right now, the left hand doesn't know what the right hand is doing. We ended up with so many leftover sausages, I brought two freezer bags full back home. My wife was not thrilled. She's been on this new health kick, trying to get me to eat turkey bacon. I told her, turkey bacon is just an insult to both turkeys and bacon. \n\nBut, uh, anyway... yeah, most of the day, nearly every day, it's just that heavy feeling. Just going through the motions.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your mood has been depressed most of the day, nearly every day, rather than just feeling that heavy low feeling you described?\n\nPatient: I feel like we're going in circles here, Doc. Like I just said, I don't really know about the word \"depressed.\" To me, depressed is someone who's crying all the time and can't even function. \n\nLike my sister-in-law, Brenda. When her dog died a couple years ago—this little Shih Tzu named Barnaby—she was what I'd call depressed. Nasty little dog, by the way. Used to nip right at my ankles every time we went over there for Thanksgiving. I told my brother Frank, I said, \"Frank, if you don't train that overgrown rat, I'm wearing my steel-toed work boots in your house.\" And I actually did it one year! Wore my heavy Red Wings right on their nice new living room rug. Brenda was absolutely furious. She had just ordered that rug from one of those home goods catalogs, spent a fortune on it. She didn't speak to me until Easter. But when that dog finally kicked the bucket, she was a total wreck. Crying all day, wouldn't leave the house, the whole nine yards. \n\nThat's depressed. I'm not doing all that. For me, it's really just that heavy, dragging feeling I was telling you about.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, would you say that you’ve experienced times when you couldn’t feel any pleasure or interest in things you usually enjoy, even for a few days at a time?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Like I was saying earlier with the grilling, if it's something I like, I still enjoy it. I don't just suddenly lose interest in it for days at a time. \n\nLike my bowling league. I bowl every Thursday night down at Strike Zone. Still look forward to it every single week. Though, I gotta tell you, last week was a total mess. We're in the middle of the fourth frame, right? We're up against the guys from the plumbing supply warehouse. And the pinsetter on lane 7 just completely jams. The sweeper bar gets stuck halfway down. So we're standing there for forty minutes waiting for the mechanic to come fix it. And the kid they got working the back now—he can't be more than eighteen, got his headphones in, barely even looking at the machine. \n\nI told Gary, he's the manager there, I said, \"Gary, you're paying this kid by the hour to stare at a conveyor belt.\" Back when I was younger, before I got my CDL and started driving, I used to do some maintenance work at a roller rink, and you actually had to hustle. You couldn't just stand around. Anyway, the whole delay completely threw my rhythm off. I rolled a 135. Usually I'm hitting at least a 180, 190 on a good night. My buddy Mike wouldn't let me hear the end of it. \n\nBut, uh... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, losing pleasure in things. No, I still like doing my stuff. The fun things are still fun. It's really just that day-to-day heavy feeling I was talking about.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, would you say that during the past few years, there were times when the things you used to enjoy didn’t feel pleasurable at all, even for a few days in a row?\n\nPatient: Wait, didn't you just ask me that? Or something real close to it? Like I said, no, I don't get like that. If I like doing something, I still like it. It's not like I just wake up one day and hate grilling or bowling. \n\nSpeaking of grilling, though, I was at the hardware store this weekend looking at those new pellet smokers. Have you seen those things? You can control the temperature from an app on your phone now. It's ridiculous. I was talking to the guy in the apron, asking him how the auger feeds the pellets, because I was thinking, if that jams up while you're at work, your whole brisket is ruined. And brisket is not cheap right now. Have you seen meat prices lately? I went to the butcher down on 4th Street—Sal's, been going there for twenty years—and a decent packer brisket was almost ninety bucks. I told Sal, I said, \"Sal, am I buying the cow or just renting it?\" He didn't think it was funny. He's been real grumpy since his son decided not to take over the shop and went to culinary school instead. Wants to make fancy foams and little smears of sauce on big white plates. \n\nBut anyway, what were we talking about? Oh, right, enjoying things. Yeah, no, I still enjoy my hobbies and stuff. It's just that heavy feeling I told you about, that's all.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, would you say that there were times when the things you used to enjoy didn’t feel pleasurable at all, even if you still did them?\n\nPatient: Doc, are you pulling my leg? I swear we just went over this twice. I don't lose interest in things. I still like what I like. \n\nIt's like my truck. I've been driving the same rig for, what, six years now? A Freightliner Cascadia. Beautiful machine when she's running right. I still love getting behind the wheel, hitting the open road, you know? Though, I'll tell you, the dispatchers lately have been driving me up the wall. They put me on this new route last month, taking me all the way up through the mountains. And the weather up there? Unpredictable. Last Tuesday, I'm hauling a load of automotive parts—mostly brake pads and rotors, heavy stuff—and out of nowhere, it starts sleeting. Just sheets of ice coming down. I had to pull over at this little diner off Route 9. \n\nPlace looked like it hadn't been updated since 1985. Neon sign buzzing, vinyl booths all cracked. But let me tell you, they made the best open-faced turkey sandwich I've had in a decade. Real roasted turkey, none of that deli-sliced garbage, and gravy that actually tasted like meat. The waitress, Shirley, she told me they roast a whole bird every single morning. I said to her, \"Shirley, if I wasn't married, I'd ask you to run away with me just for this gravy.\" She just laughed, said she hears that from every trucker who comes through. \n\nBut, uh, what was I saying? Oh, right. The truck. Yeah, I still enjoy driving. I still enjoy my food. I still enjoy my bowling and my grilling. It's just that heavy, tired feeling I get, like I'm dragging a chain around all day. That's it. None of this losing pleasure stuff you keep asking about.\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you ever had periods where you truly didn’t feel any enjoyment, even from things you usually like doing, not just a heavy feeling but a complete lack of pleasure?\n\nPatient: Doc, seriously? Are you reading off a script or something? Because I feel like I'm taking crazy pills here. We've talked about this, what, four times now? No. I don't lose my enjoyment of things. If I like it, I like it. \n\nI mean, take my dog, Buster. He's a golden retriever mix, absolute goofball. I always enjoy taking him for his walk in the evenings. Even when it's raining, though I gotta say, the smell of wet dog in my truck cab is not my favorite thing. I had to buy this special upholstery cleaner just to get the smell out of the passenger seat because my wife, Linda, she let him ride up front on the way back from the vet last month. He had to get some shots, you know, the annual ones. And the vet, Dr. Henderson, he's a good guy, but he talks your ear off about his golf game. I don't even play golf. I told him, I said, \"Doc, if I wanted to walk around a big lawn hitting a little ball, I'd just buy a riding mower and a croquet set.\" \n\nLinda thought that was hilarious, but then she let Buster put his muddy paws all over the dashboard. I spent three hours on Sunday detailing the inside of that cab. Used the good armor-all wipes and everything. You really gotta take care of your vehicles, you know? It's an investment. \n\nBut... yeah. No. I don't get that complete lack of pleasure thing. I still enjoy my dog, my truck, my bowling. It's just that tired, dragging feeling I keep trying to tell you about. Can we talk about that instead?\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you ever felt like the things you do, even the ones you say you still enjoy, just feel empty or meaningless, like they don’t really matter to you anymore?\n\nPatient: Doc, I feel like we're just going in circles here. Empty? Meaningless? No, not really. I mean, bowling matters to me because it gets me out of the house and I get to see the guys. It's not like... I don't know, trying to find the meaning of life at the bowling alley. \n\nSpeaking of the alley, though, did I tell you they changed the oil pattern on the lanes last week? Totally threw my hook off. I usually throw a 15-pound Brunswick, right? Got it drilled specifically for my grip down at the pro shop by this guy, Artie. Artie’s been drilling balls since the seventies. Guy’s missing half his left index finger—says it was a band saw accident, but rumor is he owed money to the wrong people back in the day. Anyway, they laid down this new oil pattern, they call it the \"Chameleon\" or something stupid like that, and my ball just wouldn't bite the lane. I was leaving ten-pins all night. Drives me absolutely nuts when you hit the pocket perfect and that one pin just stands there mocking you. \n\nMy buddy Dave—he drives a local route for a beverage distributor—he was having the same problem. We ended up just drinking pitchers of cheap draft beer and complaining about the management. The new manager, this kid who looks like he's about twelve, he comes over and tries to tell us we're using the wrong approach. I almost lost it. I told him I've been bowling since before he was born. \n\nBut, uh, anyway. No, things don't feel empty or meaningless. I just get tired, like I said. Just that heavy feeling. Are we gonna talk about that, or are you gonna ask me about not liking things again?\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you ever felt like the things you do, even the ones you say you still enjoy, just feel empty or meaningless, like they don’t really matter to you anymore?\n\nPatient: Doc, I swear, you're asking me the exact same thing again. Are you messing with me? I just told you, no. Things don't feel empty. I don't get why we keep circling back to this. \n\nI mean, if things felt meaningless, why would I even bother getting up in the morning? Why would I bother getting the truck washed? Speaking of which, I finally found a decent truck wash off I-95. The one I used to go to, they changed owners, and the new guys? They just rush you through. Last time, they left soap residue all over the chrome exhaust stacks. I had to spend an hour buffing it out myself. Chrome's not cheap, you know? And if you let that soap sit, it ruins the finish. \n\nThe new place, though, they actually use a good wax. The guy running it, Hector, he said they import the wax from somewhere in Europe. I don't know if I believe him, but it does leave a nice shine. He’s got this setup where they do the undercarriage wash for free if you buy the premium package. I always get the premium because, with all the salt they put on the roads up north, if you don't keep that undercarriage clean, rust will eat right through the frame. I saw a guy last winter, his whole back axle just about rusted off because he never washed the salt off. \n\nBut look, no, I don't feel empty or meaningless. I just feel tired. That's it. Just tired and heavy. Can we please move on from this?\n\nDoctor: Looking back, have you ever experienced times when you truly didn’t feel any interest or pleasure in things you usually enjoy, even for a few days at a time?\n\nPatient: Doc, I... I literally just answered this. Are you even listening to me? I don't lose interest in things. I don't stop enjoying things. Not for a few days, not for a few hours. \n\nI mean, if I lost interest in things, I wouldn't care about my garden, right? And I care a lot about my garden. I spent all of Saturday putting in these new tomato plants. Beefsteaks, mostly, but I'm trying out this heirloom variety this year, Cherokee Purple. Supposed to have a really rich flavor. You gotta be careful with them, though, because they're prone to blossom end rot if you don't keep the watering consistent. My neighbor, Mrs. Gable, she overwatered hers last year and lost the whole crop. I told her, I said, \"Edna, you're drowning the poor things.\" She's a nice lady, but she doesn't know the first thing about soil drainage. She uses that cheap topsoil from the big box stores. You gotta get the good compost, work it into the dirt before you even think about planting. \n\nI get my compost from this guy down on Route 9, sells it out of the back of his pickup. Smells awful, but man, does it make the tomatoes grow. \n\nSo, no. I don't lose interest in things. I enjoy my tomatoes, I enjoy my dog, I enjoy my bowling. I'm just tired all the time. That's what I'm here for. Can we please talk about why I feel like I'm dragging a hundred-pound weight around all day?"
